Determination of the extent area of agricultural drought during the spring of 2023, while also identification of the specific regions currently suffering from drought conditions utilizing remote sensing data of high spatiotemporal resolution, in conjunction with appropriate drought indices.\nActivity 1.3. Integrate the analysis conducted in activities 1.1 and 1.2 to determine the drought affected areas requiring recovery aids and vulnerable areas necessitating prevention aids.\nActivity 1.4. Determination of the flood effected farmlands of spring of 2023 utilizing remote sensing data of high spatiotemporal resolution and existing data and observations to recovery aids.\nActivity 1.5. Prioritize recovery and prevention aids according to the conducted rapid assessment and collecting the opinion of local experts.\n\nActivity 2.1. Conduct training programs to improve the knowledge of farmers in rainwater harvesting, rainwater reservation, and supplementary irrigation technologies for 100 farmers. \nActivity 2.2. Conduct technical training programs to improve the knowledge of farmers in rainwater harvesting, rainwater reservation, and supplementary irrigation technologies for 20 local experts.\nActivity 2.3. Deliver international successful practices in rainwater harvesting, rainwater reservation, and supplementary irrigation technologies to national stakeholders.\nActivity 2.4. Provide subscription of a national application that alarms the time of supplementary and frost Protection irrigations using satellite monitoring technology and climate data for 30 farmers that have access to limited water resource and irrigation system for operating one or two irrigation events.\nActivity 2.5. Provide appropriate soil moisture sensors that alarms the time of supplementary irrigation for 30 farmers that have access to limited water resource and irrigation system for operating one or two irrigations following the delivery of the sensors application knowledge.\n\nActivity 3.1. Conduct technical training programs for 20 local experts on developing climate-based planting calendars with the aim of using late winter and early spring precipitations for rainfed agriculture and reducing irrigation turns in irrigated areas.\nActivity 3.2. Conduct training to 50 farmers on the use of developed climate-based planting calendars in activity 3.2 to determine the best planting time.\nActivity 3.3. Organizing awareness campaigns on the advantageous of conservation agriculture techniques, such as reduced tillage, crop residual managing and cover cropping to build resilience against drought and frost. \n\nActivity 4.1. Supply recommended drought/frost-resistant seeds by MAJ research institution among 250 volunteer farmers whose farmlands are in the areas determined in Activity 1.3.\nActivity 4.2. Conduct training programs for farmers on how to properly handle and plant distributed seeds in Activity 4.1 to maximize their effectiveness regarding the sowing date determined in Activity 3.2.\nActivity 4.3. Supply an appropriate sowing machine with trained operator to be available in the region for at least 15 days for being rented by farmers. \nActivity 4.4. Conduct soil tests to distribute evidence-based nutrition use instructions among 250 farmers.\nActivity 4.5. Supply an appropriate harvest machine with trained operator to be available in the region for at least 10 days for being rented by farmers. \nActivity 4.6. Develop a comprehensive monitoring and evaluation system to track the impact of Activity 4.1 on the livelihoods of the affected farmers by conducting surveys and collecting data on crop yields, income levels.\nActivity 4.7. Provide technical assistance and highlight existing incentives to encourage private sector engagement in establishment of a seed bank to ensure the long-term availability of drought/frost-resistant seeds for farmers in the region.\n\nActivity 5.1. Conduct workshops and seminars for local 30 farmers and 10 experts to share successful national and international experiences and knowledge on techniques of artificial recharge, managed aquifer recharge, and flood water harvesting.\nActivity 5.2. Organize a field visit for 30 farmers and 10 experts to visit a successful project of artificial recharge, managed aquifer recharge, and flood water harvesting.\n\nActivity 6.1. Develop a rehabilitation plan for damaged qanats, which may include repairing and replacing damaged sections, removing debris and sediment, and improving the lining and filtration systems based on survey data and visits.\nActivity 6.2. Develop a maintenance plan for qanats to prevent future damage and ensure their long-term sustainability, which may include regular cleaning, inspection, and repair.\nActivity 6.3. Train qanat operators and local communities on the importance of maintaining and rehabilitating qanats and provide them with the necessary skills and equipment to carry out maintenance and rehabilitation activities.\n\nActivity 7.1. Conduct a needs assessment to identify the agricultural infrastructures in need of rehabilitation and reinforcement and prioritize them based on their importance for the local communities.\nActivity 7.2. Develop technical guidelines and manuals on rehabilitating and reinforcing agricultural infrastructures, which include best practices, standards, and procedures for construction, maintenance, and repair.\nActivity 7.3. Provide technical training to local engineers and technicians on the guidelines and manuals developed in Activity 7.2., to enhance their capacity to carry out the rehabilitation and reinforcement activities.\n\nActivity 8.1. Conduct soil tests on determining the status of flood-induced changes in soil health and mechanistic characteristics.\nActivity 8.2. Develop training materials and modules on soil health and mechanistic characteristics, flood-induced changes, and appropriate rehabilitation strategies, which include practical exercises, case studies, and demonstrations.\nActivity 8.3. Provide technical assistance to agricultural extension workers and farmers in implementing the appropriate rehabilitation strategies, such as soil conservation measures, crop diversification, and use of organic fertilizers.\n\nActivity 9.1. Identify, procure and deliver required equipment for urgent response against water logging and ponding on agricultural lands to the local centers of MAJ agriculture services in the flood-prone areas\nActivity 9.2. Provide technical training to the staff of MAJ agriculture services on the use, maintenance, and repair of the newly acquired equipment.\n\nActivity 10.1. Explore and promote alternative income sources and job opportunities for farmers in climate disaster prone areas, with the aim of increasing their resilience to losses and improving their livelihoods. \nActivity 10.2. Conduct training sessions on vocational training, entrepreneurship development, value addition, value diversification, and market linkages to support the establishment of non-farm enterprises and the creation of off-farm employment opportunities. \nActivity 10.3. Conduct training sessions on target vulnerable and marginalized groups, such as women and youth, to ensure equitable access to income opportunities.\n\nActivity 11.1. Conduct a workshop on insurance options and financial assistance with the aim of awareness-raising to inform farmers about affordable insurance premiums and insurance claim processing. \nActivity 11.2. Explore options for providing insurance, credit, and financial services to farmers, particularly smallholders and marginalized groups, to enable them to invest in their businesses and recover from losses.\nActivity 11.3. Financial recovery aids to farmers of drought affected areas identified in activity 1.3 who are volunteers in other activities \n\nOutputs:\nOutput 1: Baseline assessment were implemented to address
